ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-25-020-XX-NRSITZ-00164_d7e4134	Federal Minister! My question is: 250/M Why has the Federal Ministry of Economic Affairs, instead of legally sufficient standards for quality assurance, transferred this task to private associations such as the GWT, GRIS or the ÖVGW, whereby companies are forced to become a member of the respective association under significant financial burdens and with the release of internal data?
ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-25-020-XX-NRSITZ-00164_d7e4161	I would ask for the reply, Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-25-020-XX-NRSITZ-00164_d7e4176	High house! Based on the current legal situation, based on the 1942 Quality Regulation, the Ministry of Economic Affairs of the Gütegemeinschaften has approved the management of a quality and quality mark registered with the Patent Office as an association mark if the Quality Community for certain products and services defines a higher product quality by means of quality directives than it is, for example, provided for in standards. A quality community represents the higher quality awareness compared to the industry average of the combined producers, who want to identify this higher quality level accordingly. Membership in such communities is entirely voluntary. They are not necessarily directly related to the Austrian Chamber of Commerce. By no means can public tendering require proof of certain product characteristics only by means of a quality mark, unless the relevant Quality Directive is notified to the EU under EU Directive 83/189, Notification Directive. The use of non-notified rules as a condition for granting supplements is clearly illegal and would result in a withdrawal. The GWT was approved by the Federal Ministry of Construction and Technology in 1976. The authorisation has been extended several times and is currently valid until 30. September 2000. During the entire approval period, no complaints have been made to the Ministry about the work of the GWT or the other quality communities mentioned in the request.

We said: Let us, since we see the net price differences so clearly, take another start and try to reach a consensus solution in order not to have to resort to the hammer of a price scheme. Can I explain the differences to the House in two minutes? – In the Puwein study, they said: With regard to all high net price differences, it is beyond dispute that Austria has perhaps the most complex infrastructure from all EU countries, as far as traffic is concerned. We were always ready to say: A net price difference of 20, or 25 sizes, is not disputed. With regard to what goes beyond that, a net price difference of about 70 sizes compared to the most important countries with which we compare ourselves, was argued as follows: 15 large sizes come from a different business situation. I would like to say once again: Even if one accepts that more can be earned by selling tobacco, by providing longer opening hours or by side-by-side transactions, it must be the objective of an appropriate structural policy that a person who operates an ordinary gas station can also live on it and is not dependent on seven ancillary acquisitions. Therefore, even if I accept this, we have always remained the point that we have too many petrol stations with below-average turnovers. A second point has been put forward, and this has slowed down the problem of competition policy. It has been argued that the price level in Austria is partly due to the fact that we have too many small service stations with substandards and too many large gas stations that are too expensive. As a result, we have been asked to raise the standards for small petrol stations in such a way that they either leave the market or become as expensive as the oligopolists. My position and the position of my house is clear on this question. It can't be an argument that I have to lead to an increase in the cost of the others. If the standards cost money, then it should be. But it cannot be that I am throwing the few who are not subject to the funnel model out of the market. Third argument: The majors have too many gas stations. – In the event that it is so and that back cover is needed on acceptance, I have said – the corresponding order has already been done, and hopefully it will continue after the week of the car; the regulation is now being drawn up – we are going to examine with a regulation on the admissibility of automatic filling stations in Austria to ensure the supply beyond the opening times. I am sure that we will get the support of this. The other competent ministries agree. In the event that you are now instructing me to act with this new measure of price rights, I should like to tell you the following. With the criteria contained in this novel, you will remove all the major obstacles to price law that have so far been: In any case, there must be an extensive calculation of the costs of the economic sectors involved. The evaluation of the economic interest has always depended on whether – I quote old findings from the Constitutional Court – the social partners agree with this prize. Instead, you are de facto introducing the international net price in a comparable country block – that can only be the internal market – as a criterion. This gives a clear regulatory requirement that does not depend on old regulatory criteria such as "we have higher costs" and "we have better systems". A word on the tax too. High house! The statistics that we have before us clearly show that, in any case, Austria is among the least taxed countries compared with the tax burden in the European Union. To this end, I should like to mention the number of Eurosuper: there we are in fifth place. In the case of diesel fuels, we are third in terms of low taxes. – I am willing to send the detailed figures to the House afterwards. I emphasize: This discussion relates to the net price level. The net price level in Austria is disproportionately high. It is not to be seen why this is so. Consider the example of Vorarlberg: On one side of the Rhine we pay much more than on the other side, although the distance from Rotterdam is equal. It is also not to be seen that there are lower prices in some funnels around Vienna than directly before the refinery. These are things that have nothing to do with market conditions. I can therefore announce to this House: If I get the intended "empowerment" by the legislator – even though we can talk a lot about the beauty of an appeal law in theory – I would like to give Professor Van der Bellen the right to do so – then we will comply with the rules of procedure. And You can expect that the appropriate measures will then be taken by my House. I think that efforts to create more market-compliant structures in Austria must continue. In this respect, I can remind you of the documents submitted to you. The market-compliant measures proposed by competition experts are not effective in the short term, whether this is their own product pipeline, whether it is a change of the carriers of the motorway service stations – for which contracts should be terminated – or whether it is – for example, this has revived competition in France and England – that each large supermarket has its own gas station in front of the city. It's not possible to do it overnight. I therefore see as a minister of the economy in the market economy in this measure only a bridge in order to show first of all how seriously the sovereign believes and secondly, to produce a pressure in terms of competition law and the framework conditions, which does not make such a measure appear necessary in the next few years. But now, in my opinion, the patience of all those involved – including those who are constantly in the fire of responsibility – has been strained long enough. Thank you.

In this area, in order to obtain certain biotopes and biotope types, which have not least resulted from the use of these areas over the years, measures such as grazing are also very targeted. - That's the one point. It is therefore not true that, when national parks are set up, it is almost the victory of the eco-fundis, which in fact do not want to change anything there, but it is about the preservation of these biotopes, which sometimes have been created only by man's use. The second prejudice in this context, which is directly linked to the fact that tourism is alleged to be excluded because of the absolute setting of environmental protection and the protection of nature. This is not the case either, but it is one of every well-run national park that there is visitor guidance systems, that, in particular as regards the example of Burgenland, there is a networking with our neighbours and that the 12 000 hectares that this national park includes on the Hungarian side are included. Ladies and gentlemen! It makes sense that there is also a National Park Commission, which serves as a hearing and discussion body in which all stakeholders, all the groups of the population, those affected by the National Park, can be involved and can be perceived as the moods that exist, and then solutions can be discussed jointly. All of this is a good way, and so we are glad that this matter can be decided here today. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-25-020-XX-NRSITZ-00164_d7e11247	To answer the urgent question, the Secretary of State spoke in the constitutional representation of Mr Edlinger Federal Minister. The answer should not exceed 20 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-25-020-XX-NRSITZ-00164_d7e11268	Mr President. High house! Before addressing your detailed questions, allow me first to present the intentions of the Federal Government in connection with this tax reform 2000, which will of course exist. The following key objectives have guided us: Firstly, the reduction of small and medium-sized incomes – I am going to be, because this was called into question, in advance, even more closely, and secondly, the improvement of the economic and employment location of Austria, and thirdly, this reform should be designed in such a way that it does not require a savings package. These were the essential requirements for this reform. And I believe that these objectives will also be achieved! [[Conjugation with the SPÖ and the Abg. Dr. Feurstein.]] Most of the relief benefits small and medium-sized incomes and, above all, families. I'd like to do this: The annual tax relief amounted to 4 000 S at a gross monthly rate of 13 000 S for a previous tax benefit of 5 272 S. This is a 2.6% reduction in gross value added. With an income of 20 000 S, the discharge is 4 060 S. This is 1.7% of the gross relation. We also need to take account of the percentage presentation and not just the absolute amounts. On the other hand, from an income of around 41 000 s, the discharge remains constant at about 7 000 s. This means that in gross terms of 70 000 s, there is only a reduction of 0.8 per cent of the gross relation. I believe that this is quite a compromise which essentially favours small incomes. [[Conjugation with the SPÖ and the Abg. Dr. Feurstein.]] It is also worth mentioning that, as you know, the re-regulation of family taxation has increased the family allowance and the amount of the allowance for children over 1998 per child by 500 s. In order to improve the economic location of Austria, it should be noted that it was one of the main objectives to promote the re-establishment of enterprises. The public fees associated with the re-establishment, i.e. registration fees and corporate taxes, are lost. In the year of the opening, the wage-based taxes are reduced by about 7 percent of the total wage. This also sets the course for future further reforms, because it sets out where there can be starting points for such redundancies. Furthermore, this discharge in total means a start-up advantage of 1 billion schilling per year for the founders of the company. I would not, Mr Böhacker, call this peanuts, as you said. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] [[Intrusion of the Abg. Böhacker.]] In the case of a wage sum of about 10 billion schillings, which are quite reasonable in the case of 20 000 new start-ups per year, this amount is due to the reduction in the total wage and other taxes linked to the start-up. I believe that this is quite a significant amount and does not deserve the designation of 'Peanuts'. The fact that you, Mr Böhacker, referred to as "Peanuts" in the case of taxpayers remains yours, I cannot agree with that of yours. [[Abg. Haigermoser: No polemics from the government bank!]] Intensive steps are also taken in the field of research and training. The apprenticeship allowance is increased to 60 000 s, and this is related to the positive completion of the apprenticeship. The training allowance and the improved deductibility of training and training costs are intended to promote the necessary qualification of Austrian workers in order to be better able to exist in international competition. All of these are essential measures to promote the employment location of Austria. Moreover, there will be no new savings package. A total relief volume of 30 billion schilling guarantees this, and that was a very essential condition for us. On the other hand, this volume of discharge is still more than 1 percent of the gross domestic product and is therefore worth mentioning. This amount, as well as the structural changes cited above, justify, as I believe, talking about a responsible reform. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Let me now come to answer the individual questions. Question 1: This tax reform particularly relieves small and medium-sized incomes. If the total relief of 17 billion schilling is distributed to the individual income groups, it is shown that by 20 000 s the income earners receive more than 60 percent of the discharge volume. So at least 11 billion schilling comes to them. Some 4 billion schillings receive incomes between 20 000 S and 40 000 S, but only the remaining 2 billion shillings increase the incomes of higher incomes. Consideration of individual discharge also shows – I have already explained – that the persons involved in small and medium-sized incomes are much more relieved than those who are in receipt of higher incomes in relation to their previous incomes. I will present this once again in the reply to questions 6 and 7. This tax reform, as I have already said, is a first step in the discharge of the labour factor. Indeed, it is a well-known fact that the re-establishment of enterprises results in considerable employment impulses, which account for more than 1 per cent of the employed per year. The Federal Government has therefore decided to work there in the discharge of the factor. This tax reform will provide for a reduction in the additional wage costs of at least 7 percentage points for newly founded companies in the year of foundation. For these companies, contributions to the Family Allowance Fund, employers' contributions to housing, contributions to statutory accident insurance and the so-called chamber cover 2 are not included. In view of these measures, it is therefore by no means possible to claim that nothing has been done in the case of additional wage costs. See this measure as a step in the right direction and as an expression of the firm will of the Federal Government to bring further reliefs in the labour factor in the direction indicated. An important step has also been taken in the more just taxation of capital. Both the extension of the speculation period for securities to two years and, above all, the now virtually complete tax collection of these profits by a new tax system will significantly improve tax justice in this area. With the alternative of reporting profits by the bank on the one hand or a flat-rate tax deduction by the bank, on the other hand, a path of a modern and effective tax approach is taken in this direction. I must accept the accusation that the greening of the tax system has not been continued in this reform. However, I would ask you to bear in mind that this is well thought out and is in the best interest. Unfortunately, in this area – I have just done this here – we have not experienced the necessary backwind through the European Union and, for reasons of location assurance, have removed further measures in this reform. However, I should like to point out that in this area a number of things have already happened in recent years and that at European level we will devote ourselves intensively to this issue. On question 2: The task of the Tax Reform Commission was to present models and concepts for fiscal reform measures. Professor Van der Bellen, you quoted that. The task could not, on the other hand, be to present the model of a tax reform. The Tax Reform Commission has fully complied with the contracts placed in this direction. After the report had been completed, political considerations and decisions could only be taken, which proposals will ultimately be implemented. It is in the nature of the fact that the proposals presented in many areas cannot all be implemented. In fact, a number of proposals have been taken up. In this respect, I cannot follow your view that nothing has been implemented. I repeat that the proposals to reduce additional wage costs will be implemented in the sense that I presented them as a step in this direction. Furthermore, the proposal for the introduction of an equity interest rate will be implemented. The measures addressed by the Reform Commission in their report were also adopted with regard to research release, apprenticeship allowance, better promotion of lifelong learning and film support by medium-sized finance companies. At least in the basic structures, the introduction of a supported pension provision is going back to the work of the Commission. A number of proposed simplification measures have been fully or at least partly taken into account, such as the introduction of sectoral and individual flat-rate payments, the exemption of turnover tax on turnover of goods in customs administrations and the exemption of more than one third of entrepreneurs from the extra-payment of turnover tax. Finally, the facilitation of the transfer of operations by an exemption amounting to 5 million schilling in relation to the inheritance and gift tax also arises from a Commission proposal. In this context, it should also be borne in mind that this tax reform completely abolishes a tax, namely the exchange rate tax. This too is based on a suggestion from the Tax Reform Commission. The political decision only amended the stronger taxation of equity gains proposed for counter-financing, but also a first step in this direction will be taken very well. Question 3: I have already pointed out, in reply to Question No 1, the entry into the reduction of additional wage costs agreed in this tax reform. I assume that workers' chamber president Tumpel welcomes this measure. However, I acknowledge that he is waiting for further steps. Question 4: You are right that we must step up our efforts in the area of pay-related taxes. They also know, however, that for Austria's economic location, the pay-related charges should not be considered isolated. The total labour costs and the development of unit labour costs are very important for this. In both areas, Austria has respectable positions. If you look at the example of Denmark, you realize that Denmark has an additional wage cost of only about 25 per cent, but the total wage costs in Denmark are higher than those in Austria. Question 5: The implementation of measures under the National Employment Action Plan is already partly part of this tax reform. I refer to the measures related to life-long learning and the threefolding of the apprenticeship allowance. Finally, the increase and redesign of research credits also serve the goal of positive employment impulses, but the cost of research in favour of doing so is largely due to the wages of the research staff. I also see a significant contribution to the fact that a comprehensive relief of the company's creation of state charges and additional labour costs is enshrined in this reform. On question 6: It is well known that the Minister of Finance has long ago proposed that the tax reduction in the context of the 2000 tax reform should be carried out in the form of an increase in the general amount of deposit in the order of just over 4,000 S, resulting in a uniform amount of relief for all taxable persons. You mentioned this in your request. This was opposed to another proposal which would have led to a reduction in income tax by 13 000 S for income above 700 000 S. An increase in income, but not to this extent, is acceptable insofar as the cold progression is somewhat stronger – not relative – with higher incomes. The collective reform, which has now been agreed, is a compromise between these two approaches and, as I believe, is still quite acceptable from a social point of view. For gross monthly income of 100 000 s, the discharge is less than 0.6 percent, but for earners of 15 000 s almost 2.3 percent of the gross relation. Question 7: How high is tax relief for a ministerial salary? – In this example, the social dimension of collective bargaining is even more evident. In the case of a ministerial salary, the discharge is about 0.3 percent, with an income of 20 000 s you have asked for, about 1.7 percent of the gross relation. [[Abg. May. Peter: What is the Secretary of State?]] – It will also be about 0.3 percent. [[Abg. Schwarzenberger: How about university professors?]] [[Abg. Böhacker: (in the direction of the Abg. May. Peter) How about hoteliers?]] Questions 8 and 9: As I have already mentioned, this tax reform has set out an important approach to greater taxation of capital through the redesign of share taxation. I would also like to say that OECD's statistics on capital taxation do not take into account that Austria has an international, justly exemplary, taxation of capital in the form of final taxation. In the case of a slightly less formal and more economically oriented view, Austria is thus better than the statistics on the taxation of property. As far as the inheritance and gift tax is concerned, this reform does not, at any rate, mean that it will not be abolished against many claims. This also provides the basis for any further reforms in this area. Question 10: Energy taxation: In this context, it is correct and important to note that since 1996, Austria has had a general energy tax, whose rates are higher than those in the Scandinavian countries than in most other countries. The following considerations suggest further increases in energy taxation in the international equation: In the current economic situation, it was close not to take any measures that would have affected the competitiveness of the Austrian economy. This objective can be achieved in principle in the case of an increase in energy taxation in two ways: Firstly, by largely exempting companies from the energy tax, as Denmark has chosen as a model. Secondly, by reducing other taxes on the costs of enterprises, such as taxes on wages, which are of course very different in the industry. Both ways lead to a unilateral burden on households. This is on average offset by income tax reduction and the so-called transfer, i.e. the ecobonus you propose, but remains an inflationary impulse. Furthermore, to the extent of the additional transfers, there is an increase in the public expenditure and tax rate. These quotas, however, are, in particular, considered too high by all the parties represented in Parliament and very often criticised. It should also be noted that the financial compensation partners will not be insignificant from increased energy taxes to be financed by a reduction in income-dependent taxes and will therefore have to be discussed with the countries and communities in connection with the re-regulation of the financial compensation next year. On the question 11: The Federal Government cannot shut its eyes to the fact that the frequented volatileness of capital is a fact that cannot be disputed away. If all advantages and disadvantages are taken into account, especially with regard to the reduction of such tax forms in international comparison, I think that the abolition of the exchange turnover tax is a measure that strengthens the Austrian financial centre and therefore makes sense for the economy as a whole. A truly meaningful taxation of capital turnover can probably only be done in the international equation today. Austria will endeavour to make substantial contributions to this in the future. Finally, I must point out that, of course, the extension of the speculation period in the sale of shares also assumes a two-year effect in favour of a longer-term retention of securities. Question 12: Some of the proposals of the Tax Reform Commission on Fees have already been made. Think about simplifying the fees for stock contracts! Further simplifications are already in the process of becoming a law, including the gradual abolition of stamp marks by replacing modern forms of payment, including the issue of passports and driving licences. I am committed to continuing this path of reform. Work on a comprehensive fee reform is already under way and with the financial compensatory partners in the next year, because there are also appropriate national fees and charges. Questions 13 to 15: On the matter of stability programme: The aim of a discharge for small and medium-sized incomes, in addition to its social significance, which is, of course, the decisive for us, also has the effect of increasing domestic demand, which is confirmed by the economic researchers and will also be reflected in tomorrow's economic report. The scepticism of economic researchers is currently mainly influenced by the international economic developments to be countered in time. In view of the international economic decline, it will be more difficult to achieve the stability objectives precisely. It is obvious that a tax reform naturally complicates the achievement of a stability objective. It is therefore also indisputable that the successful budgetary consolidation rate of the double budget 1998/99 will continue to be consistently pursued in the future and additional efforts will be required. In all areas of the state, the money of taxpayers will be sparing in the future. This concerns all sectors of expenditure and all bodies of public law. Further loads on the budget due to additional services are not feasible without counter-financing. Structural measures to secure the consolidation price must be continued. Public administration must also be guided by increased resource and performance responsibilities. A permanent mission critique, the application of modern budgetary instruments, such as flexibilizations and outlays, will continue to be necessary. Policy will also be required in the coming years to explore the expenditure on support for their efficiency and to achieve greater efficiency of all transfers. Such a responsible policy will ensure that the Maastricht criteria can be fulfilled without a new package of savings or, in order to speak with the words of your request, without "griefance III". This tax reform is a tax reform with an eye. Of course, it is the right of the opposition to criticise this tax reform. However, the critics do not agree whether this tax reform is too generous or too low. I regard this contradiction as a good sign that the Federal Government is right with the dimension of its tax reform. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-25-020-XX-NRSITZ-00164_d7e13833	President. — I call the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). – Mr State Secretary, if you please take about 10 minutes to speak.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-25-020-XX-NRSITZ-00164_d7e13855	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! The Private Broadcasting Authority is used in the context of answering this question with regard to the modification of the participation relationship, because these changes need to be announced there, and it has to examine in principle whether the relevant legal provisions are complied with by the radio operators. This will, of course, give that authority knowledge of these changes in participation. However, we assume that this information takes place within the framework of the audit activity of that authority and is therefore an act of enforcement in accordance with Article 133 Z 4 B-VG. In other words, the members of that authority shall not be bound by instructions or subject to checks within the meaning of the supervision of bodies referred to in Article 19 B-VG in the performance of their duties. The examination of these specified changes to the participation conditions is therefore carried out within the framework of the private broadcasting authority and not within the scope of the tasks entrusted to the Federal Chancellor by law. The Federal Chancellor is in no way involved in these executions, for example by means of a supervisory right. If the Regional Radio Act now transmits the implementation of this Act to the Federal Chancellor, this means in the given context only that the Federal Chancellor is obliged to ensure the establishment and functioning of the Authority. However, the influence on the authority's activity in terms of content is denied to the Federal Chancellor by the said provision of the Constitution, which is why no responsibility of the Federal Chancellor can be given to the National Council in this respect. The correctness of this view is, moreover, achieved by the recognition of the Constitutional Court of 24 February. February 1999, in which the Constitutional Court expressly states that the establishment of authorities pursuant to Article 133c(4) leaves the provision of essential state tasks to a greater extent out of the parliamentary responsibility of the bodies appointed to manage the administration. Deputies Kier! In principle, however, I should like to point out that, on the basis of Article 20(2). 4 B-VG laid down the obligation to provide information to this day-to-day request, which is to be forwarded to the Authority, as everyone is entitled to obtain information there. We assume that there are no data protection concerns, since the company's book mentioned above would provide information so that these information can also be provided by the Commission. If this is not done directly to Parliament, we will, of course, make this information available to the authority. But I say it again: We provide this as a service and not for legal relevance. I assume that questions 3 to 5 in this section can also be dealt with in this form, with in particular 4 and 5 already being answered by the answer with a no and the yes cannot be explained. I believe that in this sense we can make a further statement in writing. Thank you.

According to the case law, a collegiate authority with judicial intervention is a tribunal within the meaning of the Convention on Human Rights, i.e. an authority that also has to agree on rights. I prefer the classical three-part constitutional concept, an independent jurisdiction – in addition to enforcement and legislation. What the Commission has to discuss here is ultimately an act of case law, so that I can give your opinion: All that is covered by the judicial administration – now analogously – is subject to answer, but all that already falls within the internal sphere of the activity of this authority, for example, as it does, against which radio operators they are acting, that is a case which, in my opinion, is excluded from the interpellation. Question 3 for example: If so, what licence holders are they? If not, why not?, these are cases that are already in place in the case law, and I do not think they are liable to answer. I can say something else on this occasion: I do not think that the Commission to preserve the Regional Radio Act or the Private Broadcasting Act is working in the sense of the legislative intention. I myself have acted there as party representatives, and what this authority, i.e. the Commission, is doing to preserve the Regional Radio Act, is rather a very restrictive way of dealing with it if you express it mildly. It could also be said that it is a refusal of law. The Commission says that it is similar to the Commission's approach to safeguarding the broadcasting law, so it is only to review things that have to do with the programme content of the broadcasting operators, but to nothing else. In fact, however, the Regional Radio Act or the Private Broadcasting Act states that the Commission decides on infringements of the provisions of the Act. I must say honestly, as a lawyer and as a lawyer, I was very surprised that the Commission, in order to preserve the Regional Radio Act, shows an inappropriate modesty and says: Here we correct the will of the legislator or we reduce the content of our control in order to avoid only what has to do with programs – from me to exceed the advertising time limit and the like. We are only responsible for this, but not for infringements of information obligations or notification obligations. We're not responsible for that. . However, I have filed a constitutional court complaint in this regard, and it is, I think, to expect an answer shortly. I would like to say the following to the Liberal Forum's question: I don't see what's behind that at all. I myself am an enemy of these restrictions on participation, as they are enshrined in the law. I am already in favour of some kind of antitrust control, but the restrictions seem very arbitrary to me. In fact, it is not to be seen that in Vorarlberg a media house that controls 95 per cent of the printing media in Vorarlberg can participate with 26 per cent of the only regional radio company and in Tyrol with 10 per cent – such a high concentration there is nowhere, not anywhere around the world – while for example, a Swedish group operating a newspaper in Sweden is excluded from acting as a broadcasting operator in Austria. Please, where's the proportionality? . I have already said in the Committee and in the hearing that this mistake should not be repeated in the private TV law. But I do not fully understand the intention, because I do not think it has anything to do with liberal thought, if we demand strict compliance with regulations or the control of provisions which are certainly not carried out by a liberal thought.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-25-020-XX-NRSITZ-00164_d7e15716	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! As a low-Austrian, I will focus on the fishing landfill. A small chronicle: At the end of the 1960s, at the beginning of the 1970s, several landfill permits were issued for the gravel pit with the current name "Fischer-Deponie" on the part of the country of Lower Austria. As a result, at least 6 000 to 7 000 barrels of toxic content – CFCs, chlorinated hydrocarbons – were deposited in the pit. In the mid-70s, Dkfm bought. Fischer the landfill. Shortly thereafter, all permits for the landfill were withdrawn from the state of Lower Austria. In 1981, the public was informed for the first time via TV broadcast "Arguments" with Walter Schiejok and the current state councillor Hans Jörg Schimanek, who also received the Conrad-Lorenz Prize, that groundwater hazardous substances are stored in this fishing dump and that this is the largest known underwater groundwater lake in Europe, the Mitterendorfer Senke. Despite numerous political promises – the then Minister for the Environment, Mrs Flemming von der ÖVP, the Environment Council and current head of state of the ÖVP, Mr Erwin Pröll, made these promises live on TV – it took eight years, however, until the Ministry of the Environment commissioned the establishment of a first safeguard in the form of lock wells. At the time, the founders of these lock wells noted that these wells could only be a first step towards complying with the safety regulations. ==References====External links== Fisher in the matter of fishing landfill charges. In this process, several witnesses confirmed that the Land of Lower Austria's landfill permits had been carried out at unusual speed and by political direction. In 1992, Fischer was legally acquitted of this accusation. Now, Fischer's Federation and Land of Lower Austria complained because he had bought the landfill with sincere permits. Fischer's claim: The Land of Lower Austria had granted the former operators, Waxina, grossly negligent landfill permits, although, due to the geological nature and the nearby Mitterendorf Senke, no authorisation for a landfill at this site should have been granted. In this process, which fishermen first won in 1998, the judge Dr. Lueger confirmed the allegations of fishermen. At the time of approval, the experts in the State of Lower Austria should have known for a long time that this site is completely inappropriate for a landfill. Following the acquittal for fishermen in criminal proceedings, in 1993, the Republic's financial procuratorate sued some of the country's politicians who were responsible for issuing the depository permits – their names are quite good: Pröll, Blochberger, Ludwig, Maurer; former ÖVP politician; some are still in office – as well as the top officials responsible at that time on payment of 15 million schilling damages for the first time. [[Abg. Smolle: Rosenstingl!]] So much had the former space, a strip of about 14 meters in length, cost the Republic. Fischer himself could not be used for lack of means. In 1994, the Landesrat Hans Jörg Schimanek, responsible for water law issues in Lower Austria, took up the Causa Fischer dump. In two water law negotiations, special experts – university professor Hölzl from the Technical University of Karlsruhe, university professor Kiesel from Vienna and university professor DDr. Haider – clearly stated that there was a risk of delay in the fishing landfill and that the poison vessels buried there would have to be immediately recovered in order to avoid a non-reciprocal contamination of the Mitterendorf Senke. According to the expert opinion Schimanek decided on "risk in delay", so that according to the law, a storage of the toxins and a safeguarding of the entire remaining content would have been possible. It was followed by a call for proposals for work involving 16 national and international companies. But before the order could be awarded to the best bidder, then ÖVP Agriculture Minister Fischler, as the Supreme Water Justice Authority, denied the decision of Schimanek, which had been binding on him in this matter, and decided: There is no danger of delay in the fishing dump. ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-25-020-XX-NRSITZ-00164_d7e16981	Mr President. High house! The present report on the implementation of the EIA Act sets out a 20-month procedure for a project of a 200 megawatt heating power plant in Salzburg. This may seem to be a long time. However, I say to you that I know many infrastructure projects to be implemented under water law and under other legislation on matter, which were considerably longer. For some you can start a year instead of a month. Another project that has been examined in this respect is a ski area, namely the connection of the ski resorts at Hauser Kaibling, Planai, Hochwurzen to the Reiteralm. It is a very large ski area – some will know it here – where the procedure was only 13 months. The duration of the procedure in the case of plant permits has developed for good reason, but it has become a sort of fetish. Let us therefore speak numbers. The study by Professor Grün, a very serious study commissioned by the Association of Industry and other studies commissioned by Mrs Langthaler, shows that in the case of some 500 installations, the average duration of approval has been only 157 days, i.e. 5 months, and that the duration of the proceedings has been significantly reduced in recent years. Of course, we should get even better, and procedures should become slimmer, cheaper and unbureaucratic, but the situation in Austria is not so bad in this regard. On the other hand, it should be noted that, in the course of the implementation of EU law, we ourselves have become sparse as a result of the implementation of the EIA-Novelle. ==References== March was the deadline, and the current situation is in fact not unproblematic. In my view, the direct application of the European EIA Directive requires a case-by-case check for hundreds of industrial plants, including small and medium-sized ones, ladies and gentlemen, because there are no national thresholds, so that it is only by individual case checks that it is necessary to determine whether this brewery or the dairy EIA is obliged or not. So there is a state of legal uncertainty that we should end as soon as possible. That is why Ministers Molterer, Farnleitner and I have drawn up a draft work on modern environmental plant law. At the last meeting of the Council of Ministers, the Chancellor made it clear that this law was to be forwarded to the Government in April or at the latest at the beginning of May and then, of course, to Parliament. In it we realise the principle of "a procedure, an authority, a notice", in English: One-stop shop. The duration of the procedure for the various procedures, from the very small to the very large installations, is standardised with values of zero to six months. The District Head Team should be the first contact person, and this should be the national governments in the instance. Ladies and gentlemen! It is about the fact that this law is intended to provide a balanced description of the interest of site security and the rapid and unbureaucratic approval on the one hand, but, of course, it is also intended to serve and reflect the fair consideration of environmental protection. The question is that standards in Austria should not be weakened, but that there should also be no standard strengthening on the other hand on this new environmental plant law. This is an ambitious but at the same time important objective, which is to bring about the implementation of the EIA Directive, the IPPC Directive for Medium-Sized Industrial Investments and, ultimately, the great EU Distort Directive, the Seveso Directive. – I thank you for your attention.

That means that it is also about speculation or soil extraction, especially in the city center. I do not want to make any appreciation here, but the Hollein-Haus versus the St. Stephen's Cathedral has also been equipped with a circular construction for this reason, because it was the most expensive place with which also space for shops has been created. I think that the buildings from the turn of the century, including the Art Nouveau houses, should be so valuable that we should involve them more and more in the protection of monuments, that what is worth receiving, and in addition to that – Mr Cap has already mentioned this – but very modern and also very courageous constructions, as in part it really happens in Paris. I think that at times we are not too courageous. What's worth it, we should get it. One last sentence: Mrs Minister, you have said the last time that we have had the renovations often for so long, because there are too few traditional craftsmen. I think it is really necessary, especially in times of job shortages or too few jobs, to go back to craftsmanship traditions and quality and to support young people. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-25-020-XX-NRSITZ-00164_d7e19754	The next speaker is Mr Dietachmayr. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-03-25-020-XX-NRSITZ-00164_d7e19775	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! A major contribution of this cultural report concerns the protection of monuments. My previous speaker has already said a lot about this. I too would like to focus on this part of the report. The report on monument care gives a detailed overview of the main activities of the Federal Monumental Office for the Exploration and Conservation of Austria's artistic, historical and cultural heritage. The responsibility for the protection of monument holdings has already prevailed very early in Austria. Already in 1850, the K. u.k. Central Commission for the Exploration and Conservation of the Building Monuments was created. A corresponding law, which is essentially still valid today, the Monument Protection Act, was created in 1923. Monument care is more than ever recognized as a natural obligation of our cultural state. Today, there is hardly anyone who wanted to deny the importance of monument care and monument protection. However, the difficulties lie in detail, especially in the need for daily decisions. Again and again, the preservation of a cultural monument depends very much on the extent to which it succeeds to raise awareness of the memorial value on the ground. The aim is to increase the focus of memorial care measures on timely precautions and, above all, on continuous care instead of expensive interventions. A general repair is usually also the most expensive way to preserve historical building and art monuments. It is therefore important to recognize damage in good time and also to be corrected in good time. It must be assumed that the current environmental pollution has increased ageing and decay processes. It is known that monument maintenance also requires appropriate financial resources, while it is known that the main part of it is owned by the monument owners. The public hand also costs conservation and restoration. In addition, it is also necessary to find spong-sors: Associations or foundations or individuals. However, it should be borne in mind that, with a small exception, the Monument Protection Act does not know the owner's obligation to maintain, but does not allow for any right of subsidisation. In this context, allow me a trip to the promotion sector in general: Our promotion is almost exclusively designed for renewal. Construction of housing, energy saving programmes, window sharing, roof use promotion: All of this is generously promoted. On the other hand, there is no incentive for repairs except for the modest conservation of monuments. Costs for monument care are extremely modest compared to those for theatre, museums, music care, folk culture, music schooling. However, this positioning does not in any way correspond to the actual social significance of the monument protection. This has resulted in a recent study in Upper Austria. Over 90 percent of the population think monument protection and monument care is a good to very good thing. It is forgotten that the aspect of public interest is too easily forgotten, although the costs are always only met by individuals, namely the preservers of the respective monument. I would also like to point out that restoration measures also provide important new insights on the field of architecture, but also on the fields of art and cultural history. In particular, the traditional craftsmanships and craftsmanship techniques remain and are thus promoted accordingly. How difficult the activity of the national conservators is can be seen in some examples. I cannot, of course, mention everyone because of the short period of time, but I would like to bring two examples from Upper Austria. In Hallstatt, for example, we originally gave priority to road construction very massively. Finally, one has opted for a tunnel ride, and it is known that in 1998 the ensemble of Hallstatt was appointed by UNESCO as the "World Heritage Site", which we are very proud of today. The second example is a "uncomfortable" monument, namely the Steyrer Wehrgraben, a medieval water channel for the former iron processing industry. It should be poured out after the destruction of the water right in 1965. On the other hand, there has been resistance, and it has been justified to receive this Wehrgraben. Today the old town of Steyr is nominated as a candidate for the "World Heritage". Ladies and gentlemen! There are still many such examples to be mentioned, but I cannot mention all of them here. But one thing is to be said at the end: Let us jointly prevent the daily disposal of tons of built history, parts of our country's cultural profile.
